They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Key variables include `early_lick_trials`, `auto_water_trials`, `free_water_trials`, `lick_directions`, `lick_times`, `correctness`, `gocue_time`, `delay_period`, `sample_period`, `stimulation`, and `trial_type`.\n- Raw lick and stimulation times are explicitly shifted into go-cue coordinates by subtracting per-trial go cue time. All sessions include `Camera0_side_JawTracking`, `Camera0_side_NoseTracking`, and `Camera0_side_TongueTracking`; some sessions additionally include whisker or lickport tracking.\n- Tongue/jaw/nose tracking time series are shaped `(n_frames, 3)` with columns `(x, y, likelihood)`. Example `Camera0_side_TongueTracking` description: `('tongue_x', 'tongue_y', 'tongue_likelihood')`.\n- Trial value encodings observed directly from NWB tables:\n  - `trial_instruction`: `left` / `right`\n  - `outcome`: `hit` / `miss` / `ignore`\n  - `early_lick`: `early` / `no early`\n  - `task`: `audio delay`\n- Unit annotations:\n  - `classification` contains `good` / `unlabelled` plus occasional `nan`.\n  - `unit_quality` contains `good` / `multi`.\n  - `anno_name` stores detailed Allen-style anatomical labels per unit; examples include `Caudoputamen`, `Secondary motor area, layer 5`, `Mediodorsal nucleus of thalamus`, `Midbrain reticular nucleus`.\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Neurons (total) | 272227 raw units across NWB files |\n| Neurons / session | min 493, mean 1564.52, max 3191 raw units |\n| Subjects | 28 |\n| Sessions / subject | min 3, mean 6.21, max 10 |\n| Trials (total) | 94990 |\n| Trials / session | min 264, mean 545.92, max 800 |\n\nAdditional raw-data statistics:\n- Units with `classification == \"good\"`: `69453` total, min `0`, mean `399.16`, max `923` per session.\n- Trial instruction counts across all raw trials: `right=48913`, `left=46077`.\n- Outcome counts across all raw trials: `hit=65254`, `miss=15641`, `ignore=14095`.\n- Early lick counts across all raw trials: `no early=84185`, `early=10805`.\n- Sessions with tongue tracking: `174/174`.\n- Sessions with at least one non-`N/A` photostimulation trial: `168/174`.\n- Core trial-table schema and core behavioral-event schema are identical in all `174/174` sessions.\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Expected Statistics (from papers/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Neurons (total) | 69,943 good units | “69,943 good units recorded across 173 behavioral sessions” |\n| Neurons / session | ~404 good units/session mean (69943/173) | Derived from paper totals |\n| Subjects | 28 mice | “based on data from 28 mice” |\n| Sessions / subject | 173 behavioral sessions total | “173 behavioral sessions” |\n| Trials (total) | not stated globally in text | Session average reported instead |\n| Trials / session | mean 476, range 130-785 | “average 476 (Mean; figure summary says 660 penetrations | “655 probe insertions” / “660 penetrations” |\n| Good-unit fraction | 25.9% of Kilosort2 clusters | “25.9 % of clusters reported by Kilosort2” |\n| Video->choice AUC before sample | 0.51 ± 0.06 s.d., n=106 sessions | “before the sample epoch … AUC … 0.51 ± 0.06” |\n| Video->choice AUC sample+delay | 0.66 ± 0.12 s.d., n=106 sessions | “In the sample and delay epochs, the mean AUC increased significantly (0.66 ± 0.12)” |\n| Video->choice AUC response | 0.99 ± 0.01 s.d., n=106 sessions | “Soon after the go cue, prediction saturated … 0.99 ± 0.01” |\n\n\n### Processing Details\n- Behavioral task structure from `methods.txt` / datapaper:\n  - Sample epoch: three 150 ms tones with 100 ms inter-tone intervals.\n  - Delay epoch: `1.2 s`.\n  - Go cue: `0.1 s` auditory cue marking end of delay.\n  - Response epoch / answer period: `1.5 s`.\n  - Consumption period: `1.5 s`.\n  - Trial ends after `1.5 s` without licking, followed by `250 ms` ITI.\n- Temporal alignment convention in the reference preprocessing is go-cue-centered. The code subtracts go cue time from lick times and stimulation times, and the papers discuss peri-go-cue timing throughout (`Time to go (s)`).\n- Photoinhibition timing: late-delay ALM photoinhibition lasts the final `0.5 s` of delay including `100 ms` ramp-down and ends before the go cue.\n- Method paper preprocessing for the video/neural analysis:\n  - Spikes converted to firing rates using `40 ms` bins and `3.4 ms` stride.\n  - Only side-view video frames were used.\n  - DeepLabCut markers included jaw, paws, and tongue.\n  - Marker outliers were identified using a five-sigma frame-to-frame velocity rule and imputed from nearby frames.\n  - Tongue position was imputed to its mean when occluded before the response epoch.\n- For PSTHs in the datapaper, spike counts were binned at `1 ms` then boxcar-averaged over `200 ms`; that is for figure generation, not the main preprocessed dataset used by the method paper.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- Spike sorting performed with Kilosort2; each cluster assigned 15 quality metrics.\n- Region-specific logistic-regression classifiers were trained from manually curated labels in five major area groups (cortex, striatum, thalamus, midbrain, medulla).\n- The classifier outputs defined the “good” units used in the papers.\n- Region mapping from `methods.txt`:\n  - Cortex classifier: ALM, other cortex, hippocampus, olfactory, cortical subplate.\n  - Striatum classifier: striatum, pallidum.\n  - Thalamus classifier: thalamus, hypothalamus.\n  - Midbrain classifier: midbrain, pons.\n  - Medulla classifier: medulla, cerebellum.\n- False alarm rates on held-out manually labeled units: cortex `7.8%`, striatum `6.4%`, thalamus `7.3%`, midbrain `5.5%`, medulla `4.3%`.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Early lick trials and no-response trials were excluded for analysis in the datapaper.\n- Overall behavioral performance for session inclusion was computed on control trials only, excluding early licks.\n- Sessions were selected for analysis with:\n  - performance `> 65%`\n  - at least `50` correct lick-left and `50` correct lick-right trials.\n- Many downstream analyses in the method paper further use only correct trials, or only non-stimulation “regular” trials, depending on the analysis.\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n| Choice from behavioral videos, pre-sample | ROC AUC `0.51 ± 0.06` (n=106 sessions) |\n| Choice from behavioral videos, sample+delay | ROC AUC `0.66 ± 0.12` (n=106 sessions) |\n| Choice from behavioral videos, response | ROC AUC `0.99 ± 0.01` (n=106 sessions) |\n| Single-neuron choice / movement prediction during delay | ROC AUC used as metric in method paper; neurons considered modulated at AUC `> 0.6` in some analyses |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Session count | Reference preprocessing / papers operate on analyzed sessions; this is consistent with the reference code once recoded. |\n\nFinal consistency understanding:\n- The NWB files are the raw session-level source.\n- The paper/code analyzed dataset is best approximated in NWB as: sessions with at least one good classified unit, using go-cue-centered alignment, and QC-filtered units corresponding to `classification == good`.\n- `go_start_times` is the reliable one-per-trial event; sample/delay event streams need within-trial disambiguation because of replayed epochs.\n- The method paper’s `40 ms` / `3.4 ms` neural preprocessing describes the reference analysis pipeline, but the requested decoder conversion will intentionally deviate to `50 ms` bins while preserving the same alignment and curation principles where possible.\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `units/spike_times`, `units/classification`, `acquisition/BehavioralEvents/go_start_times` | `neural` | Keep only units with `classification == \"good\"`; exclude the single zero-good-unit session; bin absolute spike times into go-aligned `[-2.5, 1.5)` windows using `50 ms` bins; convert counts to firing rates in Hz | `process_one_sess`, `process_one_area`, `sliding_histogram` | One trial array per trial, shape `(n_good_units, 80)` |\n| Task structure from papers (`sample = 0.65 s`, `delay = 1.2 s`) + go cue | `input[0]` | Compute `time_from_tone_onset_s = bin_center - (-1.85 s)` for every bin | `process_one_sess` stores sample/delay periods; go-centered alignment throughout reference code | Raw NWB sample-event streams contain replay-related extra events and are not reliable one-to-one trial markers; using the canonical `-1.85 s` tone onset is more consistent with the task definition |\n| `intervals/trials/photostim_onset`, `photostim_duration`, `start_time`, `go_start_times` | `input[1]` | Convert photostim onset/duration from trial-start coordinates to go-centered coordinates; emit binary `0/1` per bin center for photostim on/off | `process_one_sess` aligns stimulation by subtracting go cue | Keep stimulation trials because photostim is an explicit decoder input |\n| `intervals/trials/trial_instruction`, `intervals/trials/outcome`, lick events | `output[0]` (`choice`) | Encode left=`0`, right=`1`; for `hit`, use instructed side; for `miss`, use opposite side; for `ignore`, use first post-go lick side if present, else first lick side anywhere in trial if present, else fall back to instructed side | `process_one_sess` stores `trial_type`; lick-direction handling is implicit in exported behavior variables | Output is constant across time bins for a trial |\n| `intervals/trials/outcome` | `output[1]` (`outcome`) | Map `ignore -> 0`, `miss -> 1`, `hit -> 2` | Paper/task variable; used in trial filtering logic throughout code | Constant across time bins |\n| `intervals/trials/early_lick` | `output[2]` (`early_lick`) | Map `no early -> 0`, `early -> 1` | `get_regular_trial_mask` and datapaper methods | Constant across time bins |\n| `acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_TongueTracking` `(x, y, likelihood)` + timestamps | `output[3]` (`tongue_y_position`) | Clean tracking using method-paper-inspired velocity-outlier interpolation; align to go-centered bin centers using last-frame-carried-forward; compute per-session 40th/60th percentiles over all included aligned bins; discretize y into `0/1/2` | Method paper preprocessing + `align_markers_between_lims` | Time-varying output over 80 bins |\n| `subject` folder / NWB subject metadata | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | Use exact subject IDs (`sub-xxxxx`) and map each session to its subject index | N/A | Session order will follow sorted NWB file paths |\n| `units/anno_name` for good units | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | Use unique detailed anatomical annotations as region labels and map each good unit to its annotation index | Reference code keeps detailed `ccf_label` per neuron | More faithful at unit level than probe insertion target |\n| Fixed task specification | `input_names`, `output_names`, `output_values`, `metadata` | Populate names, class labels, and alignment/binning metadata exactly for the decoder task | N/A | Metadata will explicitly document the requested 50 ms deviation from reference 40 ms / 3.4 ms preprocessing |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Session inclusion**: Use all sessions with at least one good classified unit (`173` sessions). This matches the paper session count and avoids the single raw session with zero analyzable units.\n2. **Trial inclusion**: Keep all trials from included sessions that have full neural coverage for the requested `[-2.5, 1.5)` go-aligned window according to `units/obs_intervals`; within that valid-coverage set, retain stimulation, early-lick, ignore, and miss trials because they are required by the decoder inputs/outputs. This intentionally differs from the paper’s “regular trial” mask, and the difference is required by the user task.\n3. **Neural unit filter**: Use NWB `classification == \"good\"` as the closest NWB-native equivalent of the reference code’s external `goodunits` files.\n4. **Temporal anchor**: Use `go_start_times` as the unique per-trial alignment event. Do not assume one sample/delay event per trial because replayed epochs are explicitly stored in NWB.\n5. **Sample/tone onset definition**: Use the canonical tone onset at `-1.85 s` relative to go cue from the task structure (`0.65 s` sample epoch + `1.2 s` delay), because raw NWB sample-event streams contain replay-related extra events.\n6. **Input representation**: Both decoder inputs will be fully time-varying arrays of shape `(2, 80)` per trial.\n7. **Output representation**: All outputs will be stored as time-varying arrays of shape `(4, 80)` per trial; choice, outcome, and early-lick labels will be repeated across time, while tongue position varies across bins.\n8. **Ignore-trial choice edge case**: Because most ignore trials have no post-go lick (`13770 / 14095`), choice must use a documented fallback. I will use lick-derived side when available, otherwise instructed side.\n9. **Tongue alignment**: Use last-frame-carried-forward at each bin center to mirror the reference alignment scripts rather than linear interpolation.\n10. **Tongue discretization support set**: Compute per-session percentiles from the aligned tongue-y samples that actually enter the converted dataset, not from unrelated off-trial video periods.\n11. **Brain-region labels**: Use detailed `anno_name` labels per good unit rather than coarse probe targets, because they are unit-resolved and directly stored in NWB.\n12. **Required deviation from reference bins**: Use `50 ms` bins because the decoder task explicitly requires this, while preserving the reference session/unit curation and go-cue alignment principles.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Neural sanity check: for selected session/trial/unit/bin, recompute spike counts directly from raw spike times and verify converted firing rate equals `count / 0.05` with `np.allclose()`.\n- [ ] Input sanity check: for selected stimulated and non-stimulated trials, verify `time_from_tone_onset_s` and `photostim_on` against raw sample/go/stimulation event times with `np.allclose()`.\n- [ ] Output sanity check (trial-level): verify outcome and early-lick labels exactly match raw trial table values for selected trials.\n- [ ] Output sanity check (choice): verify hit/miss trials match lick- or instruction-derived choice, and explicitly inspect ignore-trial fallback behavior on selected trials.\n- [ ] Output sanity check (tongue): for selected session/trial/bin, verify aligned tongue y and discretized class match direct recomputation from raw tracking timestamps/data.\n- [ ] Coverage check: confirm all included sessions have at least 2 trials and all included trials have 80 aligned bins with no NaNs.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n- Implemented `convert_data.py` with CLI:\n  - Binning:\n     - Reference method-paper preprocessing uses `40 ms` bins with `3.4 ms` stride.\n     - Conversion uses `50 ms` non-overlapping bins because that is an explicit task requirement.\n   - Video / tongue processing:\n     - (https://doi.org/10.25378/janelia.24066108.v1). In brief, we used 15 cluster quality metrics to train classiﬁers - one for each of the major ﬁve brain areas (cortex, striatum, thalamus, midbrain and medulla). The classiﬁers were trained based on labels derived from manual curation. We curated Kilosort2 output (28 penetrations from ﬁve brain areas) using the open-source graphical user interface Phy (GitHub - cortex-lab/phy: phy: interactive visualization and manual spike sorting of large-scale ephys data). Each cluster was labeled as a ‘good’ or ‘unlabeled’ unit. Annotators were blind to location, recording condition and trial type information. Based on the annotated labels of all clusters and associated quality metrics, we trained ﬁve region-speciﬁc classiﬁers using logistic regression. Applying the trained classiﬁers to the rest of the dataset provided lists of units that were labeled as ‘good’ and were used for the analysis in this paper. We applied the cortex classiﬁer to ALM and other cortex, hippocampus, olfactory and cortical subplate units; the striatum classiﬁer to striatum and pallidum units; the thalamus classiﬁer to thalamus and hypothalamus units; the midbrain classiﬁer to midbrain and pons units; the medulla classiﬁer to medulla and cerebellum units. We calculated the rate at which the classiﬁer incorrectly labeled ’unlabeled’ units as ’good’, using 10-fold cross-validation on held-out manually labeled units (false alarm rate): 7.8%; striatum: 6.4%; thalamus: 7.3%; midbrain: 5.5%; medulla 4.3%. This method yielded 8717 good units from ALM, 7664 from striatum, 12808 from thalamus, 7495 from midbrain and 2928 from medulla, etc. Overall, the dataset consisted of 69,943 good units recorded across 173 behavioral sessions, from which 655 probe insertions were made. This corresponds to 25.9 % of clusters reported by Kilosort2. This is to deal with cases where for e.g. (bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride is actually 43 but evaluates to 42.999999... due to numerical errors in floating-point arithmetic.\\n    45→    if np.allclose((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride, math.floor((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride)+1):\\n    46→        n_bins =  math.floor((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride)+2\\n    47→    else:\\n    48→        n_bins = math.floor((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride)+1\\n    49→    binCenters = bin_begin_time + np.arange(n_bins)*stride\\n    50→\\n    51→    binIntervals = np.vstack((binCenters-bin_width/2, binCenters+bin_width/2)).T\\n    52→\\n    53→    # Count number of spikes for each sliding bin\\n    54→    binSpikes = np.asarray([[[np.sum(np.all([trial>=binInt[0], trial<binInt[1]], axis=0)) for binInt in binIntervals] for trial in unit]\\n    55→        for unit in spikeTimes]).swapaxes(0,-1)\\n    56→\\n    57→    if rate:\\n    58→        return binCenters, binSpikes/float(bin_width) # get firing rates\\n    59→    return binCenters, binSpikes\\n    60→\\n    61→\\n    62→def process_one_sess(data_folder, file_path_list, bw, stride, begin_time=-3.0, end_time=3.5, save_folder=os.path.join(os.pardir, 'data_processed'), qc_mode='classifier'):\\n    63→    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    64→    Process all probe files in one session.\\n    65→\\n    66→    Args:\\n    67→        data_folder (str): the folder containing all data including QC neuron ID files.\\n    68→        file_path_list: list of full file paths of all probes in this session.\\n    69→        bw (float): in sec.\\n    70→        stride (float): in sec.\\n    71→        qc_mode (str): 'classifier' or 'old'.\\n    72→    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    73→    print(qc_mode)\\n    74→    if qc_mode == 'classifier':\\n    75→        qc_folder = os.path.join(data_folder, 'goodunits')\\n    76→        print(qc_folder)\\n    77→    elif qc_mode == 'old':\\n    78→        qc_folder = os.path.join(data_folder, 'DJ_GoodUnitsIdx_14regions_old_qc')\\n    79→    else:\\n    80→        raise ValueError('Wrong qc_mode! Must be \\\"classifier\\\" or \\\"old\\\".')\\n    81→    \\n    82→    assert os.path.exists(qc_folder)\\n    83→\\n    84→    spike_times_sess = []\\n    85→    unit_info_sess = []\\n    86→    unit_qc_sess_list = []\\n    87→    ccf_coordinate_sess = []\\n    88→    ccf_label_sess = []\\n    89→    ccf_unit_id_sess = []\\n    90→\\n    91→    # ----- 1. concatenate all neurons from all probes -----\\n    92→    for i_file, file_path in enumerate(sorted(file_path_list)):\\n    93→        filename = os.path.split(file_path)[-1]\\n    94→        name_list = filename[:-4].split('_') # ['map-export, 'SC038', '20191119', '115109', 's4', 'p1']\\n    95→        mouse = name_list[1] # 'SC038'\\n    96→        date = name_list[2] # '20191119'\\n    97→        number = name_list[3]\\n    98→        session = int(name_list[4][1:]) # 4\\n    99→        probe = int(name_list[5][1:]) # 1\\n   100→        sess_name = '%s_%s_%d' % (mouse, date, session)\\n   101→        sess_name_qc = '_'.join([mouse, date, number]) # 'SC038_20191119_115109'\\n   102→\\n   103→        save_folder_sess = os.path.join(save_folder, 'stride%d_bw%d'%(stride*1000, bw*1000), sess_name)\\n   104→        if not os.path.exists(save_folder_sess):\\n   105→            os.makedirs(save_folder_sess)\\n   106→\\n   107→        if os.path.exists(os.path.join(save_folder_sess, 'others_finished.txt')):\\n   108→            print('%s has been processed. Skip.' % sess_name)\\n   109→            return # HERE\\n   110→\\n   111→        print('Reading probe file', probe)\\n   112→        # sess_file = utils.loadmat(os.path.join(file_path))\\n   113→        sess_file = preprocessing_utils.loadmat(file_path)\\n   114→\\n   115→        # --- 1.1 behaviors and tasks ---\\n   116→        auto_learn_trials = sess_file['behavior_auto_learn'] # ndarray of str, '1' is autolearn, '4' is non-autolearn.\\n   117→        auto_learn_trials = np.asarray(auto_learn_trials, dtype=int)\\n   118→        early_lick_trials = sess_file['behavior_early_report'] # ndarray, (n_trials,), int64\\n   119→        auto_water_trials = sess_file['behavior_is_auto_water'] # ndarray, (n_trials,), int64\\n   120→        free_water_trials = sess_file['behavior_is_free_water'] # ndarray, (n_trials,), int64\\n   121→        lick_directions = sess_file['behavior_lick_directions'] # ndarray, (n_trials). Each element is a list of 0 (lick left) and 1 (lick right)\\n   122→        lick_times = sess_file['behavior_lick_times'] # lick_times[trial_id] is ndarray, each element is a list of lick times (need to subtract go cue time from it)\\n   123→        correctness = sess_file['behavior_report'] # ndarray, (n_trials,), int64. 1: correct or free water, 0: error, -1: no response\\n   124→        gocue_time = sess_file['task_cue_time'] # ndarray, (2,n_trials). First row is go cue time, second row is duration of response period\\n   125→        delay_time = sess_file['task_delay_time'] # ndarray, (2,n_trials). First row is delay time, second row is duration of delay period\\n   126→        sample_time = sess_file['task_sample_time'] # ndarray, (2,n_trials). First row is sample time, second row is duration of sample period\\n   127→        delay_period = delay_time[1,:] # only need duration of delay period\\n   128→        sample_period = sample_time[1,:] # only need duration of sample period\\n   129→        stimulation = sess_file['task_stimulation'] # ndarray, (n_trials,4). The 4 columns are [laser_power, stim_type('1', '2', or '6', with 1,2,6 being left/right/both ALM perturbation), laser_on_time, laser_off_time] (with reference to trial start time - need to subtract go cue time from it).\\n   130→        trial_type = sess_file['task_trial_type'] # ndarray, (1,n_trials), 'l' or 'r'\\n   131→\\n   132→        # align stimulation time and lick times to gocue_time=0 #\\n   133→        lick_time_cal = []\\n   134→        for i_trial, lick_times_trial in enumerate(lick_times):\\n   135→            gocue_time_trial = gocue_time[0, i_trial]\\n   136→            lick_time_cal.append(lick_times_trial - gocue_time_trial)\\n   137→            stimulation[i_trial, 2:] -= gocue_time_trial\\n   138→        lick_times = lick_time_cal\\n   139→\\n   140→        if i_file == 0:\\n   141→            ### only save behavior and task info for the first probe file ###\\n   142→            sess_dict = {}\\n   143→            sess_dict['sess_name'] = sess_name\\n   144→            sess_dict['auto_learn_trials'] = auto_learn_trials\\n   145→            sess_dict['early_lick_trials'] = early_lick_trials # ndarray, (n_trials,), int64\\n   146→            sess_dict['auto_water_trials'] = auto_water_trials # ndarray, (n_trials,), int64\\n   147→            sess_dict['free_water_trials'] = free_water_trials # ndarray, (n_trials,), int64\\n   148→            sess_dict['lick_directions'] = lick_directions # ndarray, (n_trials). Each element is a list of 0 (lick right) and 1 (lick left)\\n   149→            sess_dict['lick_times'] = lick_time_cal # list with length n_trials, each element is 1D ndarray with all lick times in that trial\\n   150→            sess_dict['gocue_time'] = gocue_time[0,:]\\n   151→            sess_dict['correctness'] = correctness # ndarray, (n_trials,), int64. 1: correct or free water, 0: error, -1: no response\\n   152→            sess_dict['delay_period'] = delay_period # (n_trials,) duration of delay period\\n   153→            sess_dict['sample_period'] = sample_period # (n_trials,) duration of sample period\\n   154→            sess_dict['stimulation'] = stimulation # ndarray, (n_trials,4). The 4 columns are [laser_power, stim_type('1', '2', or '6', with 1,2,6 being left/right/both ALM perturbation), laser_on_time, laser_off_time]. For no stim trials, we have [0, NaN, NaN, NaN]\\n   155→            sess_dict['trial_type'] = 1*(trial_type=='l') # ndarray, (n_trials,). 1 is left, 0 is right 'r'\\n   156→            # with open(os.path.join(save_folder_sess, 'behavior_task.pickle'), 'wb') as f:\\n   157→            #     pickle.dump(sess_dict, f)\\n   158→\\n   159→            ### Read the neuron IDs after QC for this session ###\\n   160→            '''\\n   161→            The index refers to within individual SESSION, resulting in summing all units from all penetrations in a given Session. For instance, if in Session 08/17/22, we have 3 penetrations: p1 has 10 units, p2 has 30 units, p3 has 5 units (before filtering) - overall there are 45 units. An index of '42' from this session means the 2nd (42-10-30) unit from p3.\\n   162→            '''\\n   163→            temp_list = list(glob.glob(os.path.join(qc_folder, '{}*.mat'.format(sess_name_qc))))\\n   164→            assert len(temp_list) == 1\\n   165→            filepath_qc = temp_list[0]\\n   166→\\n   167→            qc_file = preprocessing_utils.loadmat(filepath_qc)\\n   168→            # idx_qc_dict = qc_file['Idx'] # dict. Keys are 'region_qc' (e.g. 'ALM_qc'). Values are list of good neuron IDs (in MATLAB it starts from 1 but here we change it to start from 0).\\n   169→            idx_qc_dict = {}# dict. Keys are 'region_qc' (e.g. 'ALM_qc'). Values are list of good neuron IDs (in MATLAB it starts from 1 but here we change it to start from 0).\\n   170→            for area, id_list in qc_file['Idx'].items():\\n   171→                if id_list == []:\\n   172→                    idx_qc_dict[area] = []\\n   173→                elif isinstance(id_list, int) or isinstance(id_list, float): # only one neuron\\n   174→                    idx_qc_dict[area] = [id_list-1]\\n   175→                else:\\n   176→                    idx_qc_dict[area] = list(np.array(id_list) - 1)\\n   177→\\n   178→            anno_qc_dict = {}\\n   179→            for name, value in qc_file['AnnoName'].items():\\n   180→                if isinstance(value, str):\\n   181→                    anno_qc_dict[name] = [value]\\n   182→                else:\\n   183→                    anno_qc_dict[name] = value\\n   184→\\n   185→        # --- 1.2 neural data ---\\n   186→        # -- 1.2.1 spike times\\n   187→        spike_times = sess_file['neuron_single_units'] # ndarray. spike_times[neuron_id][trial_id] is ndarray\\n   188→\\n   189→        # -- 1.2.2 unit_info and QC stats\\n   190→        unit_info = sess_file['neuron_unit_info'] # ndarray (n_units, 8)\\n   191→        # neuron_unit_info: columns: unit_id, unit_quality, unit_x_in_um, depth_in_um, associated_electrode, shank, cell_type, recording_location. For putative type, we have: 'Pyr' - pyramidal neurons, 'FS' - fast spiking, 'not classified', 'all' - all types\\n   192→        probe_no_col = probe * np.ones((unit_info.shape[0], 1)) # (n_units,1) original probe #\\n   193→        unit_info = np.concatenate([unit_info, probe_no_col], axis=1) # (n_units, 9)\\n   194→        unit_qc = sess_file['neuron_unit_quality_control']\\n   195→        # dictionary. Needed keys: 'unit_amp', 'presence_ratio', 'amplitude_cutoff', 'isi_violation', 'unit_snr', 'avg_firing_rate', 'drift_metric'. Values are all list with length n_neurons.\\n   196→\\n   197→        # -- 1.2.3 histology\\n   198→        histology = sess_file['histology'] # dictionary, keys: 'unit', 'ccf_x', 'ccf_y', 'ccf_z', 'annotation'\\n   199→        ccf_unit_id = np.array(histology['unit']) # ndarray, (n_neurons,)\\n   200→        ccf_coordinate = np.stack([histology['ccf_x'], histology['ccf_y'], histology['ccf_z']]).swapaxes(0,1) # ndarray (n_neurons,3), xyz coordinates\\n   201→        ccf_label = histology['annotation'] # list of np.str_\\n   202→        ccf_label = [str(x).strip() for x in ccf_label] # list of string, with both whitespace at the beginning and end of each string removed\\n   203→\\n   204→        # -- 1.2.4 get units that have both ephys and histology\\n   205→        unit_comb = set(unit_info[:,0]) & set(ccf_unit_id) # get the id of units that have both ephys and histology\\n   206→        mask_ephys = [True if x in unit_comb else False for x in unit_info[:,0]] # mask for all other data (ephys) except histology\\n   207→        mask_histology =[True if x in unit_comb else False for x in ccf_unit_id] # mask for histology only\\n   208→\\n   209→        # mask CCF info\\n   210→        ccf_coordinate = ccf_coordinate[mask_histology,:] # ndarray (n_neurons,3), xyz coordinates\\n   211→        ccf_label = list(compress(ccf_label, mask_histology)) # list of string, with both whitespace at the beginning and end of each string removed\\n   212→        ccf_unit_id = ccf_unit_id[mask_histology]\\n   213→\\n   214→        # mask ephys info\\n   215→        unit_info = unit_info[mask_ephys,:] # (n_neurons, 9)\\n   216→        spike_times = spike_times[mask_ephys]\\n   217→        for name in unit_qc.keys():\\n   218→            unit_qc[name] = list(np.array(unit_qc[name])[mask_ephys])\\n   219→\\n   220→        # --- 1.3 add probe data to sess data ---\\n   221→        spike_times_sess.append(spike_times) # ndarray. spike_times[neuron_id][trial_id] is ndarray\\n   222→        unit_info_sess.append(unit_info) # (n_units, 9)\\n   223→        unit_qc_sess_list.append(unit_qc) # dictionary. Needed keys: 'unit_amp', 'presence_ratio', 'amplitude_cutoff', 'isi_violation', 'unit_snr', 'avg_firing_rate', 'drift_metric'. Values are all list with length n_neurons.\\n   224→        ccf_coordinate_sess.append(ccf_coordinate) # ndarray (n_neurons,3), xyz coordinates\\n   225→        ccf_label_sess = ccf_label_sess + ccf_label # list\\n   226→        ccf_unit_id_sess.append(ccf_unit_id) # ndarray, (n_neurons,)\\n   227→\\n   228→    # ----- 2. combine data from all probes ---\\n   229→    spike_times_sess = np.concatenate(spike_times_sess) # ndarray. spike_times[neuron_id][trial_id] is ndarray\\n   230→    unit_info_sess = np.concatenate(unit_info_sess, axis=0) # (n_units, 9)\\n   231→\\n   232→    # uncommented\\n   233→    unit_qc_sess = {'unit_amp': [], 'presence_ratio': [], 'amplitude_cutoff': [], 'isi_violation': [], 'avg_firing_rate':[], 'drift_metric': []}\\n   234→    for probe_qc_dict in unit_qc_sess_list:\\n   235→        for qc_name in unit_qc_sess.keys():\\n   236→            unit_qc_sess[qc_name] = unit_qc_sess[qc_name] + probe_qc_dict[qc_name]\\n   237→\\n   238→    ccf_coordinate_sess = np.concatenate(ccf_coordinate_sess, axis=0) # ndarray (n_neurons,3), xyz coordinates\\n   239→    ccf_unit_id_sess = np.concatenate(ccf_unit_id_sess) # ndarray, (n_neurons,)\\n   240→\\n   241→    n_neurons = len(spike_times_sess)\\n   242→    assert unit_info_sess.shape[0] == n_neurons\\n   243→    assert len(unit_qc_sess['drift_metric']) == n_neurons\\n   244→    assert ccf_coordinate_sess.shape[0] == n_neurons\\n   245→    assert len(ccf_label_sess) == n_neurons\\n   246→    assert len(ccf_unit_id_sess) == n_neurons\\n   247→    # print('total neurons:', n_neurons)\\n   248→\\n   249→    # ----- 3. Process neurons from areas of interest and perform QC filtering -----\\n   250→    i_file = 1\\n   251→    neuron_id_used = []\\n   252→    neuron_id_all = np.arange(n_neurons) # neuron_id is continuous from 0. unit_id is the original id from DJ.\\n   253→\\n   254→    for region in ['ALM', 'Medulla', 'Midbrain', 'Striatum', 'Thalamus', 'Pons', 'Cerebellum', 'Hypothalamus', 'Hippocampus', 'Orbital', 'OtherCortex', 'Olfactory', 'CorticalSubplate', 'Pallidum']:\\n   255→        for side in ['left', 'right']:\\n   256→            area = '_'.join([side, region])\\n   257→            area_key = '{}_qc'.format(region)\\n   258→            neuron_id_area = helper_get_neuron_id_area(idx_qc_dict[area_key], anno_qc_dict[area_key], ccf_coordinate_sess, ccf_label_sess, side, region)\\n   259→\\n   260→            if len(neuron_id_area) == 0:\\n   261→                continue\\n   262→\\n   263→            print('Filtered neurons:', area, len(neuron_id_area))\\n   264→\\n   265→            # record used neurons\\n   266→            neuron_id_used = neuron_id_used + neuron_id_area\\n   267→\\n   268→            save_flag = process_one_area(sess_name, area, neuron_id_area, i_file, bw, stride, begin_time, end_time, save_folder_sess, spike_times_sess, unit_info_sess, unit_qc_sess, ccf_coordinate_sess, ccf_label_sess, ccf_unit_id_sess, sess_dict)\\n   269→\\n   270→            if save_flag:\\n   271→                i_file += 1\\n   272→\\n   273→    # ----- 4. Don't process the rest of the neurons -----\\n   274→    with open(os.path.join(save_folder_sess, 'others_finished.txt'), 'w') as f:\\n   275→        f.write('{}: finished.'.format(sess_name))\\n   276→\\n   277→    '''\\n   278→    neuron_id_others = set(neuron_id_all) - set(neuron_id_used)\\n   279→    neuron_id_others = sorted(list(neuron_id_others))\\n   280→\\n   281→    if len(neuron_id_others)==0:\\n   282→        return\\n   283→\\n   284→    print('Filtered neurons:', 'others', len(neuron_id_others))\\n   285→    process_one_area(sess_name, 'others', neuron_id_others, i_file, bw, stride, begin_time, end_time, save_folder_sess, spike_times_sess, unit_info_sess, ccf_coordinate_sess, ccf_label_sess, ccf_unit_id_sess, sess_dict)\\n   286→    '''\\n   287→\\n   288→\\n   289→def process_one_area(sess_name, area, neuron_id_area, i_file, bw, stride, begin_time, end_time, save_folder_sess, spike_times_sess, unit_info_sess, unit_qc_sess, ccf_coordinate_sess, ccf_label_sess, ccf_unit_id_sess, sess_dict):\\n   290→    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n   291→    Get all neurons in an area, extract firing rates, and then save data.\\n   292→    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n   293→    if os.path.exists(os.path.join(save_folder_sess, '%d_%s.pickle' % (i_file, area))):\\n   294→        return True\\n   295→\\n   296→    # filter using the neuron mask\\n   297→    n_units = unit_info_sess.shape[0]\\n   298→    spike_times_area = spike_times_sess[neuron_id_area]\\n   299→    unit_info_area = unit_info_sess[neuron_id_area,:] # (n_units, 9)\\n   300→\\n   301→    # uncommented\\n   302→    unit_qc_area = {}\\n   303→    for qc_name in unit_qc_sess.keys():\\n   304→        unit_qc_area[qc_name] = np.array(unit_qc_sess[qc_name])[neuron_id_area]\\n   305→        assert len(unit_qc_sess[qc_name]) == n_units\\n   306→\\n   307→    ccf_coordinate_area = ccf_coordinate_sess[neuron_id_area,:] # ndarray (n_neurons,3)\\n   308→    ccf_label_area = list(np.array(ccf_label_sess)[neuron_id_area])\\n   309→    ccf_unit_id_area = list(ccf_unit_id_sess[neuron_id_area])\\n   310→\\n   311→    # truncate spike times into desired time window (already aligned to go cue in raw data)\\n   312→    for i_cluster, st_cluster in enumerate(spike_times_area):\\n   313→        for i_trial, st_trial in enumerate(st_cluster):\\n   314→            # if spike_times[i_cluster][i_trial] != []:\\n   315→            #     amin_all.append(np.amin(spike_times[i_cluster][i_trial]))\\n   316→            #     amax_all.append(np.amax(spike_times[i_cluster][i_trial]))\\n   317→            if isinstance(st_trial, float) or isinstance(st_trial, int):\\n   318→                spike_times_area[i_cluster][i_trial] = np.array([st_trial])\\n   319→            # temp = spike_times[i_cluster][i_trial].copy()\\n   320→            temp = spike_times_area[i_cluster][i_trial] # in Susu's data, spike_times are relative to go cue time\\n   321→            spike_times_area[i_cluster][i_trial] = spike_times_area[i_cluster][i_trial][(temp>=begin_time)*(temp<=end_time)]\\n   322→\\n   323→    # bin spike times into firing rates\\n   324→    bin_centers, fr = sliding_histogram(spike_times_area, begin_time, end_time, bin_width=bw, stride=stride, rate=True) # fr: (n_bins, n_trials, n_neurons)\\n   325→\\n   326→    if os.path.exists(os.path.join(save_folder_sess, '%d_%s.pickle' % (i_file, area))):\\n   327→        with open(os.path.join(save_folder_sess, '%d_%s.pickle' % (i_file, area)), 'rb') as f: # HERE\\n   328→            area_dict_exist = pickle.load(f)\\n   329→            fr = area_dict_exist['fr']\\n   330→            spike_times_area = area_dict_exist['spike_times']\\n   331→            bin_centers = area_dict_exist['bin_centers']\\n   332→\\n   333→    # organize data into dict\\n   334→    area_dict = {}\\n   335→    area_dict['sess_name'] = sess_name # str\\n   336→    area_dict['area'] = area # str\\n   337→\\n   338→    area_dict['bin_centers'] = bin_centers\\n   339→    area_dict['fr'] = fr # (n_bins, n_trials, n_neurons)\\n   340→    area_dict['spike_times'] = spike_times_area # ndarray. spike_times[neuron_id][trial_id] is ndarray with all spike times of this neuron in this trial. (go cue time is 0.)\\n   341→    area_dict['neuron_info'] = unit_info_area # ndarray (n_units, 9)\\n   342→    #  columns: unit_id, unit_quality, unit_x_in_um, depth_in_um, associated_electrode, shank, cell_type, recording_location, probe #. For putative type, we have: 'Pyr' - pyramidal neurons, 'FS' - fast spiking, 'not classified', 'all' - all types\\n   343→    area_dict['unit_qc'] = unit_qc_area # dict. Keys are 'unit_amp', 'presence_ratio', 'amplitude_cutoff', 'isi_violation',, 'avg_firing_rate', 'drift_metric'. Values are all list with length n_neurons.\\n   344→    area_dict['ccf_coordinate'] = ccf_coordinate_area # ndarray (n_neurons,3), xyz coordinates\\n   345→    area_dict['ccf_label'] = ccf_label_area # list of string, with whitespace at the beginning and end of each string removed\\n   346→    area_dict['ccf_unit_id'] = ccf_unit_id_area # ndarray (n_neurons,), id of units with histology\\n   347→\\n   348→    for name, info in sess_dict.items():\\n   349→        area_dict[name] = info\\n   350→\\n   351→    # Empty 0x1 cell. Need to manually define what it becomes in Python.\n2. 1x1 cell. Will be directly read as non-iterables such as str, int, float, etc. Need to manually convert it to an iterable with length 1.\n3. Nx1 cell (N >= 2). Usually it's fine to read and directly store it in Python.\n\nComment: in case 2, when using isinstance(data, data_type), if data could be a number, consider the possibility of data being either float or int, i.e. use\n    if isinstance(data, float) or isinstance(data, int):\n        do something\n\"\"\"\n\nplt.rcParams['pdf.fonttype'] = 42\nplt.rcParams['ps.fonttype'] = 42\n\n\ndef sliding_histogram(spikeTimes, begin_time, end_time, bin_width, stride, rate=True):\n    '''\n    Calculates the number of spikes for each unit in each sliding bin of width bin_width, strided by stride.\n    begin_time and end_time are treated as the lower- and upper- bounds for bin centers.\n    if rate=True, calculate firing rates instead of bin spikes.\n    '''\n    bin_begin_time = begin_time\n    bin_end_time = end_time\n    # This is to deal with cases where for e.g. (bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride is actually 43 but evaluates to 42.999999... due to numerical errors in floating-point arithmetic.\n    if np.allclose((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride, math.floor((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride)+1):\n        n_bins =  math.floor((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride)+2\n    else:\n        n_bins = math.floor((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride)+1\n    binCenters = bin_begin_time + np.arange(n_bins)*stride\n\n    binIntervals = np.vstack((binCenters-bin_width/2, binCenters+bin_width/2)).T\n\n    # Count number of spikes for each sliding bin\n    binSpikes = np.asarray([[[np.sum(np.all([trial>=binInt[0], trial<binInt[1]], axis=0)) for binInt in binIntervals] for trial in unit]\n        for unit in spikeTimes]).swapaxes(0,-1)\n\n    if rate:\n        return binCenters, binSpikes/float(bin_width) # get firing rates\n    return binCenters, binSpikes\n\n\ndef process_one_sess(data_folder, file_path_list, bw, stride, begin_time=-3.0, end_time=3.5, save_folder=os.path.join(os.pardir, 'data_processed'), qc_mode='classifier'):\n    \"\"\"\n    Process all probe files in one session.\n\n    Args:\n        data_folder (str): the folder containing all data including QC neuron ID files.\n        file_path_list: list of full file paths of all probes in this session.\n        bw (float): in sec.\n        stride (float): in sec.\n        qc_mode (str): 'classifier' or 'old'.\n    \"\"\"\n    print(qc_mode)\n    if qc_mode == 'classifier':\n        qc_folder = os.path.join(data_folder, 'goodunits')\n        print(qc_folder)\n    elif qc_mode == 'old':\n        qc_folder = os.path.join(data_folder, 'DJ_GoodUnitsIdx_14regions_old_qc')\n    else:\n        raise ValueError('Wrong qc_mode! {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_31","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/bash -lc \"sed -n '1,260p' /app/methods.txt\"","aggregated_output":"Behavior and video tracking\n\nMice performed an auditory delayed response task 21 (Figure 1A). The instruction stimuli during the sample epoch were pure tones played at one of two frequencies (3 kHz or 12 kHz). Each tone was played three times for 150 ms with 100 ms inter-tone intervals. The sample epoch was followed by a 1.2 s delay epoch. An auditory ‘Go’ cue (carrier frequency of 6 kHz with 360 Hz modulating fre-\nquency, 0.1 s duration) indicated the end of the delay epoch. Licking early during the sample/delay epoch triggered a replay of the epoch. During the response epoch (answer period: 1.5 s) mice reported the instruction by licking one of the two lick ports. Licking (consumption period: 1.5 s) the correct lick port triggered a small water reward (0.1 ~ 0.2 ul). Licking the incorrect lick port triggered a timeout (1-3 s). After mice stopped licking for 1.5 s, the trial ended, followed by a 250 ms inter-trial-interval. Early lick trials and no\nresponse trials were excluded for analysis. Overall performance was computed as the fraction of correct control trials (i.e. no photostimulation), excluding any early lick trials. We selected experimental sessions for analysis based on following criteria: overall behavioral performance (> 65%), and at least 50 correct lick left and lick right trials each. Mice performed on average 476 (Mean; range, 130-785) trials per session with 84% correct rate (range, 65-99%; STAR Methods). During the delay epoch, mice maintain a memory of their choice and plan a movement.\n\nTwo CMOS cameras (CM3-U3-13Y3M, FLIR) were used to track orofacial movements of the mouse under IR light illumination (940 nm LED). The cameras were equipped with 4-12 mm focal length lenses (12VM412ASIR, Tamron) and a pixel resolution of 71 mm. High-speed videos from a side view and a bottom view (Figure 1C) were acquired at 300 Hz using software FlyCapture (TELEDYNE FLIR). We trained DeepLabCut 39 to track the movement of tongue, jaw and nose (Figure 1I).\n\nMulti-regional electrophysiological recordings\n\nWe designed a ﬂexible manipulator system for multiple Neuropixels probe insertions (https://www.janelia.org/open-science/manipulator-system-for-multiple-neuropixels-probe-recordings). Three to four small craniotomies (diameter, 1 \u0018 1.5 mm) were made over target brain areas one day before the ﬁrst recording session. Details of recordings made with multiple Neuropixels probes in head-restrained behaving animals have been published (dx.doi.org/10.17504/protocols.io.8tphwmn). Target regions, insertions angles, probe types, etc. are summarized in Table S2. All coordinates are given with respect to Bregma (AP, anterior-posterior; ML, medial-lateral; DV, dorsal-ventral).\n\nExtracellular spikes were recorded using Neuropixels 1.0 probes, 2.0 single-shank (SS) and 2.0 multi-shank (MS) probes 3,4 (Table S2). Three to seven recordings were made from each craniotomy. Recording depth was inferred from manipulator readings (Sensapax uMp manipulators). After completion of probe insertion, brain tissue was allowed to settle for several minutes before recording. During recordings the craniotomies were immersed in saline.\n\nProbes were conﬁgured and data were visualized and streamed to disk using the open-source software package SpikeGLX (https://billkarsh.github.io/SpikeGLX/). Data from up to ﬁve simultaneously recorded probes were acquired at 30 kHz. Sync waves (0.5 s duty cycle TTL pulses) were recorded across probes and on auxiliary channels for synchronization.\n\nPhotoinhibition\n\nLight from two 473 nm lasers (Coherent OBIS LS/LX) was used to illuminate the two hemispheres of ALM. The laser power was controlled by analog outputs controlled by a Bpod (Sanworks). Photoinactivation of ALM (centered on AP 2.5 mm; ML 1.5 mm) was performed through clear-skull cap implant11 or via craniotomy by directing the laser over the skull (beam diameter, 1.8 mm at 4 sigma). Photoinhibition of the left-, right-ALM hemisphere, or both hemispheres was deployed on a subset of ~25% randomly interleaved trials 11,21 (N = 17 VGAT-ChR2-EYFP mice). To prevent the mice from distinguishing photostimulation trials from control rials using visual cues, a ‘masking ﬂash’ (1 ms pulses at 10 Hz) was delivered using 470 nm LEDs in front of the mice throughout the entire trial. For silencing ALM, we stimulated cortical GABAergic neurons in VGAT-ChR2-EYFP mice. We used 40 Hz photostimulation with a sinusoidal temporal proﬁle (5 mW average power per hemisphere) and a 100 ms linear ramping down during the laser offset to reduce rebound neuronal activity. This photo stimulus is expected to silence glutamatergic neurons in all of ALM. 37 We silenced ALM activity during the late delay epoch (last 0.5 s), including the 100 ms ramp-down. Thus, photoinhibition always ended before the ‘Go’ cue.\n\nTo analyze the effects of photoinhibition, units with at least 10 photostimulation trials were selected for analysis (Figures 5 and S5). Bilateral ALM photostimulation reduced behavior performance from 83.2% to 71.7% (N = 17 VGAT-ChR2-EYFP mice, n = 93 sessions). To exclude the effects of rebound population activity in the thalamus 16 and other subcortical regions, we used a 20-120 ms\ntime window after photo stimulation onset to measure the effect of ALM inactivation in downstream regions (Figures 5 and S5). For ALM itself, we used 500 ms (i.e. the entire inactivation period) to calculate spike rate reduction.\n\nSpike sorting and quality control\n\nWe developed a data pipeline for preprocessing, spike sorting and quality control (https://github.com/jenniferColonell/ecephys_spike_sorting). The extracellular recording traces were ﬁrst band-pass ﬁltered (Butterworth acausal ﬁlter, 250 Hz - 9 kHz). We then sorted the dataset using both Kilosort2 32 (MATLAB version, GitHub - jenniferColonell/KS20_for_preprocessed_data: Release version of KS2 for preprocessed data) and Kilosort2.5 4 (Python version, GitHub - jenniferColonell/pykilosort: [WIP] Python port of Kilosort 2). Both sorting methods gave similar results. In the analyses presented in the paper, we used the output from Kilosort2. Each output cluster was associated with a list of 15 quality metrics calculated to assess isolation and sorting quality 7 (i.e. quality control) (https://github.com/AllenInstitute/ecephys_spike_sorting/tree/master/ecephys_spike_sorting/modules/quality_metrics).\n\nThe details of the spike sorting and quality control are described in an accompanying white paper (https://doi.org/10.25378/janelia.24066108.v1). In brief, we used 15 cluster quality metrics to train classiﬁers - one for each of the major ﬁve brain areas (cortex, striatum, thalamus, midbrain and medulla). The classiﬁers were trained based on labels derived from manual curation. We curated Kilosort2 output (28 penetrations from ﬁve brain areas) using the open-source graphical user interface Phy (GitHub - cortex-lab/phy: phy: interactive visualization and manual spike sorting of large-scale ephys data). Each cluster was labeled as a ‘good’ or ‘unlabeled’ unit. Annotators were blind to location, recording condition and trial type information. Based on the annotated labels of all clusters and associated quality metrics, we trained ﬁve region-speciﬁc classiﬁers using logistic regression. Applying the trained classiﬁers to the rest of the dataset provided lists of units that were labeled as ‘good’ and were used for the analysis in this paper. We applied the cortex classiﬁer to ALM and other cortex, hippocampus, olfactory and cortical subplate units; the striatum classiﬁer to striatum and pallidum units; the thalamus classiﬁer to thalamus and hypothalamus units; the midbrain classiﬁer to midbrain and pons units; the medulla classiﬁer to medulla and cerebellum units. We calculated the rate at which the classiﬁer incorrectly labeled ’unlabeled’ units as ’good’, using 10-fold cross-validation on held-out manually labeled units (false alarm rate): 7.8%; striatum: 6.4%; thalamus: 7.3%; midbrain: 5.5%; medulla 4.3%. This method yielded 8717 good units from ALM, 7664 from striatum, 12808 from thalamus, 7495 from midbrain and 2928 from medulla, etc. Overall, the dataset consisted of 69,943 good units recorded across 173 behavioral sessions, from which 655 probe insertions were made. This corresponds to 25.9 % of clusters reported by Kilosort2. \n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

The default is 5.\n    \n    Returns\n    -------\n    r2_scores_per_fold : np.ndarray\n        The R² scores for each fold.\n    y_test_per_fold : np.ndarray\n        The target data for each fold.\n    y_pred_per_fold : np.ndarray\n        The predicted data for each fold.\n    test_masks : np.ndarray\n        The trial masks for each fold.\n    '''\n\n    kf = StratifiedKFold(n_splits=cv)\n    r2_scores_per_fold = []\n    y_test_per_fold = []\n    y_pred_per_fold = []\n    test_masks = []\n\n    for train_index, test_index in kf.split(X, trial_mask):\n        X_train, X_test = X[train_index], X[test_index]\n        y_train, y_test = y[train_index], y[test_index]\n        mask_train, mask_test = trial_mask[train_index], trial_mask[test_index]\n        test_masks.append(mask_test)\n\n        model.fit(X_train, y_train)\n        y_pred = model.predict(X_test)\n        r2_scores = r2_score(y_test, y_pred, multioutput='raw_values')\n\n        r2_scores_per_fold.append(r2_scores)\n        y_test_per_fold.append(y_test)\n        y_pred_per_fold.append(y_pred)\n\n    return np.array(r2_scores_per_fold).T, np.concatenate(y_test_per_fold, axis = 0), np.concatenate(y_pred_per_fold, axis = 0), np.concatenate(test_masks, axis = 0)\n\nfind_closest = lambda x, x0: np.argmin(np.abs(x-x0))\n\ndef temporal_alignment_embed_and_ephys(ephys_tt,embed_tt, embed_vecs, fr, dt = 0.0034):\n    '''Align the embeding vectors and the firing rates in time.\n    \n    This assumes that the dt is the same for both datasets.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    ephys_tt : np.ndarray\n        The time stamps for the ephys data.\n    embed_tt : np.ndarray\n        The time stamps for the embedding vectors.\n    embed_vecs : np.ndarray [time, trial, embedding_dim]\n        The embedding vectors.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
